  ### The commits
  1. **`637a4752` The oracle.** Changes no product code.
     - `scripts/oracle/sql-scriptdom.cs`: a .NET file-based program on ScriptDom
  170.191.0, pinned. It prints errors, creates (with the CREATE's line, and
  whether the script or a module's body runs it), every schema-object name and
  how it is used, and the strings executed.
     - `scripts/oracle/sql-scriptdom.ts` runs it and keeps records.
  `scripts/oracle/sql-compare.ts` is the comparison; its header states the
  grading rules once.
     - New: the `oracle:sql` npm script, the fixture
  `tests/integration/fixtures/sql-server/`, kept records for that fixture and
  for the estate's SQL Server scripts,
  `tests/integration/live-docs/sql-oracle.test.ts` (with a register of measured
  misses), `scripts/oracle/sql-compare.test.ts`, and a row in internal-tooling.
  2. **`b26cb98f` The crash.** A name in brackets or double quotes is passed
  over whole, with `]]` and `""` as escapes. A string or comment left open runs
  to the end of the text. A name is split only at dots outside its quotes. It is
  written back bare unless a part holds `.`, `[`, `]`, `"` or a space at either
  end; then that part is bracketed.
  3. **`27e53ad7` The lines.** The blanking keeps line breaks.
  4. **`fdf9533e` Tokens.** The reader is now a tokenizer. These are no longer
  read as objects: a CTE, an UPDATE's or DELETE's alias, a cursor read by `FETCH
  … FROM`, `CREATE TYPE/USER … FROM`, a foreign key's `ON UPDATE CASCADE`,
  T-SQL's reserved words, and the text inside a bracketed name.
  5. **`44c8dcfe` The engine's own catalog.** `sys.*`, `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.*`,
  and one-part `OPENJSON`, `STRING_SPLIT` and `GENERATE_SERIES` are no longer
  kept as names. **This revises the owner's October 9 rule ("no builtins",
  2026-10-09 record, Turn 10).** It stands alone so it can be dropped. On tSQLt
  these were 207 of the 213 wrongly kept names left at that point.
  6. **`c47bd69f` Tables made in a procedure's body.** A table created inside a
  procedure, function or trigger body is no longer a symbol of the script,
  though it still counts as the file's own. This is my decision, for the owner
  to confirm. ScriptDom places such tables in the body, and on tSQLt there were
  374 of them, linking test files to each other. The price: if an estate's
  procedure creates a permanent table at run time and no script defines that
  table, other scripts' uses of it become kept names instead of links to the
  procedure.
  7. **`e46f6253` The missing references.** Now read:
     - calls by two or more parts;
     - `EXEC @rc =`, `APPLY`, a MERGE's `USING`, `REFERENCES`;
     - INSERT, DELETE and MERGE without their optional INTO or FROM;
     - comma lists of tables, and tables in parentheses.

     Now set aside: xml-type methods on a column, event-session events and
  targets, a Service Broker service, a table variable's alias, PL/SQL's `EXECUTE
  IMMEDIATE`.

     A lone carriage return now ends a line. The adapter reads each file once,
  and name matching is indexed by last part. That took its second pass over
  DNN's provider scripts from 19 s to 3 s.
  8. **`04ca9ad0`** Adds the oracle's two entry points to
  `tests/integration/live-docs/expected/repository-entries.json`. The gate found
  them missing; this belongs with commit 1.

  ### A fork I decided that you may judge differently
  The brief said to stop if a mend changed another language's reading. The C#
  adapter hands the SQL in its strings to this same reader, so every mend
  applies there too. I judged that to be the SQL reading itself and went ahead.
  I measured C# docs in this repository, the estate and the cold run's shop,
  before and after: none changed. I did not regenerate DNN's C#.

  ### Other forks I decided
  - The written-name form described in commit 2.
  - A call is a name of two or more parts followed by `(`, unless the word
  before it is ON, INTO, FUNCTION, INDEX, EVENT, TARGET or a similar word naming
  the thing.
  - A CTE's scope is its whole batch, not just its statement. No case where this
  matters was found in any of the four corpora.
  - T-SQL's nested block comments are read as T-SQL reads them.
  - The comparison grades the uses a map should draw. Drop, grant, ALTER
  PROCEDURE, type, sequence, synonym target, trigger and index tables, and
  SELECT INTO are counted, not graded.

  Open questions for the owner:
  - Should a literal executed string be read as SQL?
  - Should ALTER of an uncreated object make a symbol?
  - Their `OPENQUERY(ORACLE_CENTRAL, '…')` "OraQueries" are the same question,
  on the wire that matters most.

  ### PL/SQL today
  - **Right:**
    - standalone `CREATE [OR REPLACE] PROCEDURE|FUNCTION|VIEW|TABLE` at the
  right line;
    - `/` ends a batch;
    - FROM, JOIN, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, MERGE, REFERENCES and cursor SELECTs
  are read;
    - `SYS.` names are set aside.
  - **Not symbols:** packages, specs and bodies, and the procedures inside them;
  triggers, sequences, synonyms, types.
  - **Calls:**
    - Package calls (`audit_pkg.log_event(…)`) are read as calls and kept as
  names.
    - One-part calls (`log_line(…)`) are not read.
    - `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` is not read; until commit 7 it produced a dependency
  named `IMMEDIATE`.
  - **Break everything after them:** a SQL*Plus `PROMPT` or `REM` line
  containing an apostrophe, and q-quoted strings (`q'[it's]'`).
  - **Differs from Oracle:** comments are read nested; bare `FILE`, `KEY` and
  `PLAN` are not read as names (T-SQL reserves them).

  ### UTF-16
  The adapter reads the file as UTF-8, finds nothing, and says nothing: no
  symbols, no dependencies. ScriptDom, which reads the byte-order mark, sees the
  objects.

  ### Unfinished
  - The format's crash 2, as above.
  - No symbol kind exists for TYPE, TRIGGER, SYNONYM or SEQUENCE: 9 objects on
  tSQLt and 4 on DNN. Synonyms occur only inside test bodies here, but an estate
  may reach Oracle through them. A new kind would touch the board's door kinds
  and the Explorer.
  - The implicit EXEC (a procedure called without `EXEC` as the first statement
  of a batch) is not built. It would need a list of unreserved statement words
  such as THROW and PROMPT.
  - `.SqlDataProvider` files are not read.
  - Speed: about 2.8 s per pass over DNN's 6.6 MB of provider scripts. The
  original reader took 6.4 s to read them twice. On tSQLt a pass takes about 0.5
  s, about twice the original's.

  ## Summary

  All three readers of a markdown link's target now use one rule, CommonMark's,
  in one new module. Nothing in this repository's map moved, and the Explorer
  now opens what the engine counts. The work is one commit, `c2bfd65f`, on my
  worktree branch, on top of `e5e28301`. It is not pushed and nothing was
  amended.

  **Over time.** I handed back about an hour late; your later message (the names
  review) arrived near the end, and most of it is not done (see the last
  section).

  **Shared files I touched outside the brief:**
  - one line in `package.json`, the sample's build command;
  - one entry in `scripts/e2e-bundles.mjs`;
  - one line in `tests/integration/live-docs/expected/repository-entries.json`,
  the entry points expectation.

  The full write-up is in `AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-1
  1-19/Probes/markdown-link-targets/README.md`, committed and beginning "Current
  as of November 19, 2026."

  ## Where the module lives

  `packages/engine/src/tooling/markdownLinks.ts`, beside `githubSlugger.ts`,
  which the client bundle already imports from this folder.
  - Its only import is `tooling/htmlEntities.ts`, HTML's 2,125 named references.
  I took them from the MIT `html-entities` package already among the dev
  dependencies.
  - `relatedDocs.ts` stays apart from `graph.ts`, so the glob matcher still
  stays out of the browser.
  - It exports three things:
    - `readMarkdownLinks(document)` returns every link, image and autolink with
  offsets, kind, destination and title, plus the references whose label nothing
  defines, plus the definitions.
    - `inlineTokens(text)` gives the renderer one paragraph's links, code spans,
  escapes, character references and raw HTML.
    - `linkPath(destination)` sets the fragment apart, then the query, then
  percent-decodes the path. A path with malformed encoding is left as written.
  - It differs from CommonMark in three places, all named in its header: it does
  not recognise indented code, fences inside a list or quote, or HTML blocks.
  The cause is the same for all three: it keeps no container structure.

  ## Oracle and score

  The oracle is CommonMark 0.31.2's published examples
  (`https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/spec.json`). No parser was among the
  dependencies, so I did not add the `commonmark` package.
  - I kept the eight sections that bear on links (backslash escapes, references,
  definitions, code spans, links, images, autolinks, raw HTML): 230 examples.
  They sit in
  `packages/engine/src/tooling/__fixtures__/commonmark-link-examples.json` with
  their source and licence, CC BY-SA 4.0.
  - `markdownLinks.commonmark.test.ts` compares each example's `<a href>` and
  `<img src>`, titles included, after commonmark.js's URL normalization. Every
  named difference must still fail, or the test fails.

  | Reader | All 230 | Links (90) | Backslash escapes (13) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | New rule (destinations and titles) | **229** | 90 | 13 |
  | Old engine (destinations only) | 124 | 34 | 9 |
  | Old Explorer | 108 | 24 | 9 |
  | Old audit | 131 | 39 | 9 |

  The one miss is example 211, a definition inside an indented code block, which
  the rule reads because it does not recognise indented code.

  ## Reference-style links and autolinks: counts and a fork for you

  Counted with the new rule:

  | Corpus | Files | Reference links | Relative | Autolinks | Relative |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | This repository, every tracked `.md` | 1,992 | 0 | 0 | 3 | 0 |
  | Dnn.Platform, its own markdown | 104 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
  | The cold corpora's Live Docs, plus dbatools, shop and tSQLt's own markdown |
  about 13,000 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

  How each reader treated them before:
  - The engine counted neither.
  - The Explorer linked http/mailto autolinks and showed reference links as
  text.
  - The audit checked reference links, reported undefined labels, and skipped
  autolinks.

  The module reads references without a second pass: definitions sit at the
  start of a paragraph, and they are collected while splitting lines into
  paragraphs, a step inline links already need.

  The engine still counts only inline links and images. Autolinks are absolute
  URLs, so they can never be related documents.

  **Fork: should the engine count reference links?** I left them uncounted
  because the reader renders authored sections one at a time. A reference whose
  definition sits in another section would be counted but would not open.
  Counting them takes two changes:
  - the renderer hides definition lines;
  - `detailPanel.ts`, which is not mine, passes the whole block's definitions to
  `renderMarkdown`.

  My recommendation is to make both changes and count them. Nothing would move
  today, since no corpus holds a relative one.

  ## Review subagent

  I used one: a general-purpose agent of my tier, `a0a50988723d7766a`. It found
  three problems, all fixed and tested:
  - **Forgeable held piece.** A link's address could carry U+E000, the character
  that marks the renderer's held pieces. That let it break out of an attribute
  or recurse until the page crashed. This was a regression of this round.
  - **`javascript:` through the default handler.** The default link handler
  could be led to write a `javascript:` address with `java&#9;script:`.
  - **CRLF offset.** In CRLF documents, a link ending a line had an end offset
  one character too far.

  What it found that I left is listed below.

  ## Unfinished

  - **`graph.ts`:** `resolveDocLink` should read `linkPath(link).path` from the
  module instead of `document.ts`'s `decodeLinkPath(link.split("#", 2)[0])`.
  That also sets the query apart, and `decodeLinkPath` could then go. Until then
  the engine and the audit differ only on a raw `?` in a relative link, and
  none exists here.
  - **`reader-links.ts`** (lines 16 to 31) keeps its own copy of the decoding.
  The client can import the module, as `markdown.ts` now does. I did not change
  it; it is not my file.
  - **Other readers still on their own patterns:**
    - `scripts/slopcop/symbolReferences.ts` (uses `decodeURI`);
    - `scripts/doc-tools/documentationLinks.ts` (uses `decodeURI`; part of
  `verify`);
    - `scripts/live-docs/lint.ts:262`;
    - `githubSlugger.ts:116`.
  - **Block-level differences the review found.** A list item not numbered 1, or
  an empty item, ends a paragraph here. A `--` setext underline is not seen. A
  lone `=` line is skipped. Nested container markers are not stripped. U+0000 is
  not replaced. All are rare, and none occurs in this repository's links.
  - **Quadratic slow inputs the review measured.** All need adversarial
  paragraphs.
    - `"[a](".repeat(n)` takes 34 s at 80 KB, a regression for the engine.
    - Unclosed `<!--`, `<?` and `<!a` constructs.
    - Many images, many rendered links, or many unmatched `[` in one paragraph.
  - **The audit's line numbers in CR-only files.** Every issue lands on line 1,
  because `computeLineStarts` in `markdownShared.ts` counts only `\n`.

  ## Your later message (the names review), point by point

  1. **The audit's `decodeURI`:** covered. It now uses `linkPath`, which is
  `decodeURIComponent`. `%23` and `%3F` are tested; `%26` decodes the same way
  but has no test of its own.
  2. **`symbolReferences.ts` and `documentationLinks.ts`:** not done.

  ## 1. Directives

  **What changed**
  - The adapter now reads `.ascx`, `.master`, `.ashx`, `.asmx`, `.asax` and
  `.svc`.
  - It follows every attribute Microsoft documents as naming a file, on Page,

  Reference, MasterType, PreviousPageType and Assembly.

  - The `~/` rule is built exactly as ruled.
  - **Where a path finds no file:** where several web projects match, none does,
  or the path starts at the site root (`/`), there is no link and the written
  path is kept by name.
  - **The reason is not said yet.** The doc format has no way to say why a

  **Decision for you: what `~/` should resolve to when the own project and
  another web project both hold the file.**
  - **(a) Keep the ruling.** Accept the stub wires.
  - **(b) No link when several web projects hold the path, own project
  included.**

  link, 6 of them right wires lost.
    - DNN's sample is unchanged.

  closest to the truth.

  I recommend (b) now, and (c) only if the owner's estate deploys this way. The
  estate sample has no case of this.

  ## 4. The puzzle: why DNN draws 5 of 134 designer pairs and mojoPortal 277 of
  308

  **Cause:** this is not type choice among namespaces. DNN writes every member
  access as `this.member`, by StyleCop's SA1101 rule. The C# reader already does
  candidate A for bare names: it links a partial half to the peer whose members
  it uses. But a chain that starts with `this` is never followed, so
  `cmdSave.Click` is recorded and `this.cmdSave.Click` is not.

  **Evidence, over every designer pair** (pairs / drawn today):

  | How the code-behind names a designer-only member | DNN | mojoPortal |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Bare, at least once | 2 / 2 | 276 / 276 |
  | Only through `this.` | 107 / 2 | 3 / 0 |
  | Not at all | 25 / 1 | 29 / 1 |

  Following `this.` completes candidate A and should draw about 107 more pairs
  on DNN.

  ### Red, then green, per item
  - **Item 1:** a new round-trip test saves two docs with CR-only endings and
  authored words, deletes one doc's source, and runs the generator.
    - Before: the kept doc's words were replaced by the placeholders.
    - With only `authoredBlockOf` mended, generation then stopped on the
  preserved CR-only doc ("the text must end with a newline"). So the `Reader`
  and the comparison now take a lone CR too. That is safe because `UNWRITTEN`
  quotes any CR inside a name.
    - The fuzz and grammar tests also read every doc with CR-only endings.
  - **Item 4:** the barrel test now also declares `FOO`.
    - Before: `Foo` / `foo` were `symbol-foo` / `symbol-foo-1`, colliding with
  `FOO`.

  ### Fuzz widening (`e2ba6d9b`)
  - A path segment may now begin with any character (a quote, a space, `#`, a
  dot) and keep a lone surrogate. Such a link must be written quoted and still
  resolve.
  - Half the docs stand at the workspace root, so a link can begin with neither
  `./` nor `../`.
  - The pieces now include `&amp;` and `&#x26;`, and the chosen names add
  `Q&amp;A`, `R&D` and `"q"`.
  - It passes as it stands.

  ### Item 6, `SOURCE` and `LINK`: left as two slots
  `SOURCE`'s plain form admits a `)` and a `#` and is read up to the last
  `#L<n>)`. `LINK`'s admits no `)`. So older docs' raw source links with
  parentheses (Next.js route groups) read today; merged into `LINK` they would
  be refused.

  ### Item 3, the CommonMark reference parser (probed, not committed)
  I installed `commonmark` 0.31.2 (the latest) to probe, then took it back out
  of `package.json` and the lock, because a green oracle needs your rulings
  first. Its line is `"commonmark": "^0.31.2"`. What it shows:

  1. **A quoted name is not inert. This is a real hole in our writer.**
     - `#### "<img src=x>\n"` is raw HTML.
     - `#### "[a](https://e.invalid)\n"` is a live link.
     - **Proposed mend:** write the quoted form inside a code span, i.e. the
  JSON string as the span's content, and quote every name whose value begins
  with `"`.
     - **Cost:** quoted names haven't landed anywhere yet, so that part is free.
  But names beginning with `"` change form, and the reader's `""` vs `"\"\""`
  distinction becomes `` `""` `` vs `` `"\"\""` ``.
  2. **Titles and `Code Path` are raw paths.**
     - Eight docs of this repository render a title bold on GitHub
  (`tests/integration/programs/python/**/__init__.py` → **init**).
     - A file `<b>x</b>.ts` gives raw HTML; a file `[a](b).ts` gives a link.
     - Mending moves docs: backslash-escaping punctuation would move those 8; a
  code span would move all 1,092.
  3. **Docstrings are markdown by intent.** A `# heading`, a setext underline or
  a `[a](b)` link in a docstring renders as such. "No heading or link arising
  from a docstring" would mean escaping docstring markdown in general.

  All three are recorded under "What remains" in the decision.

  ### What moved in this repository's docs
  - After every commit, `--check` reports 1,092 unchanged.
  - Each regeneration moved only the docs of files that commit edited (line
  numbers, a new import or parameter): `document.ts`, `compose.ts`,
  `graphFiles.ts`, `generator.ts`, `document.test.ts`, the round-trip test.

  ### Gate (under a private `TMPDIR=/tmp/ldn3.jfus`, since removed)
  - Load average was 18.5 at the start and 22.7 at the end.
  - Passed: Live Docs pipeline, lint, build, type-check of tests, unit
  (2,255/2,255), integration (158/158), documentation link enforcement.
  - It failed only at the SlopCop markdown audit, on three links to the review,
  which this branch lacks; `5225ffd4` fixes those.
  - I then ran the three audits on their own: `slopcop:markdown` 0 broken,
  `slopcop:symbols` 0 issues, `slopcop:assets` 0 broken.
  - No timing failure this run.

  ### Words (item 7)
  - **Header:** `document.ts`'s header now says
  `renderLiveDoc(parseLiveDoc(text)) === text` holds for docs this version
  writes. An older doc may be refused or, in two cases, read as another value;
  `generate` rewrites it.
  - **The decision's "What moved"** now lists:
    - **Refused:** a padded name in a slot that used bare backticks, and a code
  path beginning with `"`.
    - **Read silently as another value:** a docstring line with a backslash
  before structure, and a raw link with a well-formed `%XX`.
    - **Re-formed:** names holding controls or separators; link paths with
  space, `%`, `&`, `?`, `<`, `>`, `\`, controls or parentheses; and `(kind)` on
  a name ending in a parenthesised word.
    - **Upgrading:** a repository with older docs runs `live-docs:generate`
  first; the reader does not accept the older forms.
  - **"Deliberate"** now records the lone-surrogate link written as a JSON
  string, and the heading that packs name and suffix together (a separate suffix
  slot was considered and not chosen).
  - **"What remains"** now names `%26` alongside `%23` and `%3F` for the audits,
  the Explorer renderer's gaps, `find-orphans.ts`, and the CommonMark findings
  above.

  ### Not done

  ## Step 2 is built and committed as `cb427f0b` on my branch

  It sits on top of `8d31ff04`. Nothing is pushed, nothing is amended, and I did

  **Gate:** `npm run safe:commit` without the browser, under a private TMPDIR,

  oracle among them) all passed.

  README. I fixed the anchor, reran it, and it is clean.
  - `livedocs --check` is clean. Lint warns of 60 disconnected files. Four of

  (`Own.ps1`, `Copies/Use-Neither.ps1`, `Backport/Use-Archive.ps1`,
  `scripts/Clean.ps1`).

  ## What is built, per your rulings

  All in `packages/engine/src/live-docs/adapters/powershell.ts`:

  - **Rule R, "loaded, else unique".**
    - Only definitions at a file's own level count.
    - A name the caller defines itself is its own.
    - Otherwise the call goes to the one defining file the caller's loading
  reaches. Loading means its dot-sources, imports and `using module`, followed,
  plus its modules' files and their loads. A script run by its path is not
  followed.
    - If loading reaches several definitions, no wire. If it reaches none, the
  call goes to the only file in the workspace that defines the name.
  - **Loads the text fixes**, each drawn as a wire of its own:
    - `Join-Path` in both parameter forms;
    - a variable assigned once by `=` at the file's own level;
    - `foreach` or `ForEach-Object` over a `Get-ChildItem` of a fixed folder,
  directly or through such variables;
    - a script run by its path.
  - **Shipped names are blocked only at the fallback.** The list is in
  `adapters/powershell.builtins.ts`, 444 names:
    - Windows PowerShell 5.1: from Microsoft's reference,
  `MicrosoftDocs/PowerShell-Docs` `reference/5.1` at commit `aedd8efe`, 366
  commands in 18 modules.
    - PowerShell 7.7.0: from `Get-Command` over the modules under `$PSHOME` on
  Linux, 288 commands. The Windows-only ones are covered by the 5.1 list.
    - The sources are named in the code, the test and its Live Doc.
  - **The lenient library rule:** a `.ps1` whose own level only defines things
  and sets up its scope is a library, not `Entry: program`.
  - **`FunctionsToExport` uses the existing barrel mechanism.** It is returned
  as `reExportedSymbols`, which the generator renders as "Re-Exported Symbol
  Anchors" linked to each origin file. No new mechanism and no wire.
  - **Every PowerShell file is parsed once per run**, into a table kept per file
  index the way C# keeps its type table. A file's own analysis reuses its
  facts. Before, each file was parsed twice.

  ## The oracle

  - The emitter (`powershell-ast.ps1`) is committed with its Live Doc. It now
  records commands, file-level definitions, statements and the new loads; loop
  listings come from PowerShell's own `Get-ChildItem`.
  - The rules are written a second time over the parser's facts in
  `scripts/oracle/powershell-wires.ts`, sharing no code with the adapter (only
  the shipped-names list, which is data).
  - `powershell-oracle.test.ts` now grades calls, loads, the program/library
  mark and manifest offers on three fixtures. The new one is
  `tests/integration/fixtures/powershell-wires`, with a README of its shapes.
  - The register holds one miss, and it is the grammar's: it reads
  `./scripts/Clean.ps1` as the dot-source `. /scripts/Clean.ps1`.

  assignment (`[SuppressMessageAttribute(...)]` on the line before `$x = …`).

  ## Forks for you

  1. **Variables assigned inside a block.** In 14 AL-Go test files,
  `$scriptRoot` is assigned inside `BeforeAll` and used in the same block.
  That's 138 loads your "assigned once at file level" ruling leaves unread.
  Extending it to "assigned once in the file, in the block that holds the load"
  stays text-fixed.
  2. **R's known weakness.** A test that stubs an outside module's command at
  its own level becomes that command's only definition. The oracle draws 5 such
  false calls in AL-Go (`Tests/RunTests.Test.ps1` stubbing BcContainerHelper);
  the adapter happens not to, because the grammar reads those lines inside an
  error. Nothing is built for this.
  3. **Breadth of the shipped list.** It covers every module PowerShell installs
  with itself, including ISE, PSReadLine, PackageManagement, PowerShellGet and
  PSResourceGet. Narrow it if you prefer the core modules only.
  4. **Not read:** `Split-Path` forms and loops over a written-out list of
  names.

  ## Candidates for the default (default unchanged at 1,024)
  1. **Keep 1,024**: same picture everywhere; `tests` stays closed on the
  owner's machine for no reason it has.
  2. **2,048**: every folder opens here, no cost on the card; 3 fps without any
  card; unmeasured on a built-in card.
  3. **All**: same here, but no ceiling on a ten-thousand-file repository. I
  would not ship it.
  4. **Page chooses by the renderer's name** (processor: 1,024; else 2,048):
  stable per machine; a sent link opens differently on another machine; some
  browsers hide the name.
  5. **Page chooses by measuring** (not built): needs a measure, a rule with
  slack, a visible statement of the limit in force, the same fact through
  commands, pinned browser cases. My own readings of one picture ranged 47 to 58
  fps under other load, so the same link could open differently for the same
  person on two visits; the limit would be a fact from the machine, not from the
  Live Docs.

  **My recommendation:** keep 1,024 shipped until a machine with only a built-in
  card is measured (two minutes with the lever); the owner sets 2,048 on their
  own machine. If the page must choose, by the renderer's name, not by
  measuring.

  ## Writing a link to a folder as a link: what it would take
  The tree writer would create a real symbolic link only when `followLink`
  resolves it to a folder inside the tree. It would refuse any such link at,
  above or beneath the docs root, because the generator writes Live Docs under
  the base layer: a link like `.mdmd/layer-4/src -> ../../src` would put docs
  into the sources. The existing check that nothing is written beneath a link
  already stops tree entries from passing through it.

  With that, imports and probes would resolve through the link as in a checkout,
  and discovery would not walk into it, since its walk leaves directory links
  unfollowed. History would then equal a checkout. What could go wrong:
  - **Cycles.** A link to an ancestor (`a/loop -> ..`) makes endless paths. Any
  reader that walks while following links could run long or forever.
  TypeScript's `readDirectory` for a `tsconfig` without `include` is one such
  walker; it needs a check.
  - **Escaping the tree.** The target must still lie inside the tree after full
  resolution, judged by the commit's listing, not the disk.
  - **Windows without the right to make links.** `symlink` fails with `EPERM`. A
  junction works for folders without that right, but then the history can
  differ by platform, which the lens does not key. Leaving such a link out and
  naming it is the safe fallback.
  - **A second way through the disk.** Every writer added later would need the
  docs-root guard.

  ## What moved in this repository's docs
  - New Live Docs: the three files of the C fixture (authored purpose and notes
  written) and `summarizeChange.test.ts`.
  - Generated sections changed for `git.ts`, `regenerate.ts`,

● Your answers are acted on, and your four design questions turn out to be one
  question. I have a recommendation, and a builder is measuring tonight what I
  don't yet know.

  Acted on

  - packages/cli: I'll delete it next.
  - Node 26: it becomes the project's Node when the engine round lands, because
    a Node change also forces a history rebuild and should share that one. The
    timer workaround goes.
  - OPENQUERY to Oracle: the SQL builder now treats it as the connection that
    matters most. You will probably need to say once which folder a linked
    server's name stands for; I'll bring where that lives.
  - .sql only: nothing to build. Oracle packages are being measured anyway. The
    ~/ rule stands.

  The one question: what does a person run, and where are the facts kept?

  On October 3 we proposed, and never settled, "an HTML file with no project
  documented in it whatsoever, which opens right up to the world map with one
  node, asking you to point it to a directory to start documenting." Yesterday
  you described the same first piece "begging to be pointed somewhere". My
  recommendation follows from that:

  1. A scan is one folder you point at, and a piece on the World Map is one
     scan. Your work board would have a piece per repository: the PowerShell
     scripts, each .NET solution, each database. Connections between pieces are
     found by matching, such as an OPENQUERY naming a table the Oracle
     repository declares, or you declare them. The estate sample already works
     this way with seven folders.
  2. You're right that our own World Map doesn't. Its pieces are folders carved
     by hand out of a single scan (packages/engine, packages/explorer, and so
     on). Under this rule our repository is one piece.
  3. So "where is another folder's Explorer built" was my wrong question. There
     is one Explorer per board, not per folder. One folder alone is a board with
     one piece.
  4. The typical user runs the page, not Node. They open the file, pick a
     folder, and it scans in the browser. Nothing is written unless they ask,
     and the page can save a copy of itself, blank or with the map inside. The
     command line stays for teams who want docs committed and checked, and for
     agents.

  For your work machine this is concrete: running the tool today needs Node plus
  a checkout of about 2.7 GB, of which 2.2 GB is our own session archive.

  What I don't know yet, and am having measured before proposing an order of
  work:
  - whether a page opened from disk can read a picked folder in Edge or Chrome;
  - how long and how much memory 1,790 PowerShell scripts take in a tab;
  - what the file weighs (in October: about 14 MB to view plus 16 MB to scan);
  - which parts of the generator need Node (history, which reads git, is the
    hard one);
  - the smallest command-line install, for comparison.

  Your hypothesis about the authored section

  I counted in our own docs:

  - 1,090 of 1,093 Live Docs have an authored Purpose.
  - 382 source files already open with a comment saying what they are for, and
    every one of those also has an authored Purpose. We say it twice for a third
    of the repository.
  - About 100 docs are for files that can't hold a comment: 72 JSON, 12
    pictures, 4 sounds.

  What it would buy: docs become fully derivable from the code, so nothing has
  to be written into a repository. That removes the "3K+ UNCOMMITTED FILES"
  problem and the disk cost, and frees the storage format.

  What it would lose:
  - words about files with no comment syntax;
  - words about code you can't or shouldn't edit, such as your database
    repositories or someone else's code;
  - long notes on why a file exists, which don't belong in a source header.

  All three have a remedy that already exists: a markdown document that links to
  a file shows up with that file. A README beside a folder of pictures
  describes them; our screenshot folders work that way today.

  My leaning, not yet a proposal: you're right in direction, with one
  adjustment. Keep the Live Doc as the form every view reads and can export as
  markdown, but stop requiring the mirror on disk. Writing docs into a
  repository becomes a team's choice. This changes the vision's "Markdown is
  canonical", which is yours to change. Before proposing it properly I'll bring
  two comparisons: what the 382 opening comments say against their authored
  Purposes, and what our Notes hold that has no other home.

  Next

  Delete packages/cli, then look at the 2D and 3D trial pictures and the engine
  round's latest mends, all of which came back while I was writing. Fourteen
  builders are out.

  **Cause:** a tie in the placement, not tie-breaking by numbering.
  - `compose.test.ts`'s single pin has wires of equal weight above and below it,
  so its cost is flat between two pins. `graph.test.ts` does the same, 368 px
  up and back.
  - The network simplex starts from a placement where everything stands as near
  the top as its constraints allow. It moves only while a move lowers the cost,
  so it stops at the first equal-cost place it reaches.
  - A levelling joins two outline runs into one node, which can change that
  starting placement. The card then lands at the other end.

  **Smallest reproductions:**
  - `smallest-system.ts`, five nodes: `x` stands on `p` under all 120
  numberings, so numbering is not the cause. Adding one constraint that never
  binds (x ≥ r+100, with r held by nothing else) puts `x` on `q`, 72 px lower,
  at the same cost of 72,072.
  - `smallest-tie.ts`: a card wired to four pins. Adding a wireless card above
  it moves it 70 px down at equal cost.

  **Mend:**
  - The solver takes an optional second objective (`Constraint.tie`). Cut values
  become pairs compared in dictionary order. There is no big multiplier, so
  integers stay exact. Without ties it pivots exactly as before.
  - `placeBranches` takes `anchor` (the shown picture's tops) and wires each
  item to one origin through the second objective only. These links are kept out
  of the component alignment.
  - `layoutScene` gains an optional sixth argument, `anchor`.

  **Tests:**
  - Two tests are red against `f0ddaef8`'s solver and placement and green now. I
  showed this by setting the two files aside with a uniquely tagged stash,
  applying it back by its SHA, then dropping it.
  - One harness case: every node of all 43 harness systems (40 seeded, plus the
  3 real ones) is drawn to random places. Cost, optimality and feasibility equal
  the tie-free solve on every one. It has a 60 s timeout, because the
  1,500-node systems take about 2 s each under load.

  **Counts at the defaults (levellings anchored):**
  - All 207 trials keep their price, and the same 13 moves happen at the same
  prices.
  - Cards moved per move go from 29,1,91,29,1,86,2,1,2,6,1,2,1 (252 total) to
  28,0,91,28,0,87,0,1,2,5,0,1,0 (243 total).
  - `compose.test.ts` moves at 2 moves instead of 9: order 5 (30 px), and move 6
  (7 px, with 86 other cards).
  - `graph.test.ts`'s 368 px shuttle is gone.

  **Counts at 80 orders:**
  | Mode | Trial prices that differ | Moves | Cards moved | Moves of
  `compose.test.ts` |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Today | – | 29 | 506 | 12 |
  | Levellings anchored | 0 of 311 | 29 | 493 | 4 |
  | Every trial anchored, orders too | 15 of 311 | 29 | 485 | 4 |

  **Not every trial:** anchoring every trial made those 15 order trials cost 200
  more (one more outline step); all were refused either way. The placement's
  cost cannot differ, but the price adds two things the placement does not
  weigh: the count of outline steps and the picture's height. So the version to
  land is levellings only. It changes no price on either setting.

  **Speed:** the search ran 8–9 % slower with levellings anchored, measured side
  by side at load 22–32.

  **The seam I need in `branch-renderer.ts` (not mine):** pass the shown tops as
  `layoutScene`'s sixth argument for levellings.
  - Line 1264 (`searchStep`) and line 1385 (`tryAdoptPicture`): append `, kind
  === "alignment" ? controller.previousTops ?? undefined : undefined`.
  - Land the lab's matching change in the same commit: `lab-seam.diff` in the
  probe folder (`evaluateAlignment` anchors to its previous tops). I kept it out
  of the lab until the page anchors, so `local-map-simplicity.spec.ts` still
  compares like with like.
  - **Option:** use `search.incumbent.scene.placement.top` instead of
  `controller.previousTops`, which also holds the lane slots. At 80 orders that
  still changes 0 of 311 prices, and the six card-less late levellings then move
  0 slots (today 6–17 each; 1,354 → 1,177 slot moves in the whole search). It
  is untimed side by side.
  - No browser case pins the 80-order end, so no truth needs updating.

  ### Fault 2: levellings that lower the price and worsen the picture (measured,
  price unchanged)
  **What moved:** in each of moves 23, 24, 25, 28 and 29, exactly one term
  changed: the outline step count, −1, worth −200 at Membrane Simplicity.
  Vertical wire, crossings, height, width, evenness pixels, row levelness and
  the ranking terms were all unchanged, and no card moved.

  | Move | Outline height added (all empty ground) | Outlines changed |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 23 | +180 px | `scripts/live-docs` and `inspect` |
  | 24 | +300 px | `packages` and `packages/engine/src` |
  | 25 | +72 px | `explorer/src/shared` |
  | 28 | +608 px | `scripts` |
  | 29 | +608 px | `scripts/live-docs` |

  **Why:** evenness prices |a−b|+|b−c|. On an outline running one way that
  equals |a−c| however many steps it takes, so levelling one stair costs
  nothing. Nothing in the price weighs enclosed ground: the placement weighs
  outline height at 1 against a wire pixel's 1,000,000, and the price not at
  all.

  **Candidates (which of moves 23 / 24 / 25 / 28 / 29 each would refuse):**
  - **A.** Count steps only between columns that both hold the directory's own
  cards. Refuses 24, 28, 29; keeps 23 and 25.
  - **B.** Price outline height per pixel. Thresholds to refuse 23 / 24 / 25 /
  28 / 29: above 1.11 / 0.67 / 2.78 / 0.33 / 0.33. At 2 or more it overrides
  Membrane Evenness's deliberate stretching (2 per px of step, the Oct 7
  design).
  - **C.** A levelling that moves no card or line is not a trial. Refuses all
  five plus move 22, and keeps the estate's (it moves a card). This is a judge
  rule, in the other round's files, not a price change. Once fault 1 lands it
  also refuses defaults moves 2, 5, 7, 11 and 13, leaving 8 moves instead of 13.
  - **D.** Price a name standing apart from its first card. Covers 23 and 24
  only.

  **Not measured:** the estate's L→rectangle levelling and `pathfind.ts`. The
  lab captures pinned files only, and those two pictures need directories opened
  or files outside its four fixed scopes. The table says what follows from the
  packet's descriptions.

  ### Fault 3: the first order (measured only, partial)
  - The first picture is seed 2's, priced 3,445,009. Order 77 alone is
  3,200,099, which is −7.11 %.
  - **Where the gain is:** vertical wire −245,107, plus crossings −300 (−24,000)
  and height −1,075 px (−5,375). Against that, the outlines get worse: steps'
  pixels +10,871 (+21,742), steps −5 (−1,000) and row levelness +4,415 (+8,830).
  - **What it reorders:** columns 4–8 completely (13, 27, 13, 9 and 2 cards;
  none kept). Columns 4–7 rise to begin at 241, 86, 197 and 1,558 px, where they
  began at 666, 1,321, 2,954 and 2,876. The 27-card column now ends at 8,187
  instead of 9,392: that column is the packet's long tail.
  - **How rare:** of 82 orders, 17 beat the first. Sixteen of them are two
  pictures at −0.46 % and −0.45 %; only order 77 is large.
  - **Cheap rules:** sweeping the first picture's starts 8, 16 or 32 times gives
  the identical picture. A seed takes 411–1,130 ms (median 549) at load 16–22.
  - **Not done:** the 30-map seeded sample (it needs a browser capture per map,
  about 1 minute each through the lane), and the median rule or a neighbour-swap
  pass in place of the barycenter.

  ### Other findings
  - The lab's `evaluate` routes every trial, which made a `refine`-style replay
  take over 15 minutes. `lean.ts` avoids that.
  - Lane slots also shuttle or move in card-less levellings. The packet counted
  few lines redrawn, and I did not trace why.

  ## 4. `this.` chains
  **Test seen failing:** a new case in `csharp.test.ts` where a code-behind
  reaches its designer's members only through `this.cmdSave` and
  `(this).lblTitle`.

  **What changed:** in `csharp.ts`, `this.member` and `(this).member` now count
  as uses of a member of this type, in partial types only.
  - `base.member` and `((Base)this).member` deliberately do not count, and the
  test checks both. A member reached through `base` belongs to the base type,
  which the base list already links. A member reached through a cast belongs to
  the cast's type, which its type name already links.
  - **`this.field.Method()`** records only `field`, as a peer-member use.
  - **Grammar detail:** in the tree-sitter C# grammar `this` is an anonymous
  keyword node, so the parenthesised form has to look through all children, not
  just the named ones.

  **Code-behind → designer links:**

  | | Pairs | Before | After |
  | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |

  | mojoPortal | 308 | 277 | 280 |

  lost:

  **How it was measured:** one Roslyn compilation per project, built from syntax
  trees and referencing the compilations of its `ProjectReference`s, with no
  framework references. Namespaces are not counted as edges. Without the

  **Ours that the compiler lacks, by cause:**

  limit of the oracle) | 243 | 0 |

  | 141 | 21 |

  172 |

  | A member of a partial peer | 1 | 0 |

  **The finding.** The cold run's "95% resolved" counts lines that found a file,
  not the right file. On mojoPortal about one in six C# dependency lines points
  at the wrong file (887 certainly wrong, 376 very likely).
  - **Why:** mojoPortal has one data-layer project per database, and each
  declares the same `mojoPortal.Data.ConnectionString`, `DBFields` and so on.
  `SuperFlexiData.MSSQL/DBFields.cs` references only the MSSQL project, yet our
  reader also links it to the MySQL, SQLite and pgsql copies.
  - **The cause in the reader:** it resolves a full type name across the whole
  workspace and never narrows by the projects the file's own project references.
  - **Relevance to the owner:** the estate's Portal and Gateway both declare
  `Controllers.PaymentsController`, which is the same shape in miniature.

  Three of your four rulings are built and committed. The fourth (keep the

  ## The rulings

  Each ruling below has a test that failed against `cb427f0b` first: four tests
  failed there and pass now. Each is also written a second time in the oracle
  (`powershell-ast.ps1` and `powershell-wires.ts`). The wires fixture gained a
  case of each, and the adapter and the oracle agree on all of them; the
  register still holds only the `./Clean.ps1` miss. The other two fixtures'
  oracle records are unchanged.

  ### 1. Variables assigned inside a block

  Built as you worded it: assigned exactly once in the file, by `=`, before the
  load, in the block holding the load or one enclosing it.

  - **The measure barely moved.** The adapter gained no wire. The oracle gained
  two, in `Tests/RunTests.Action.Test.ps1`, a file the grammar cannot read in
  full.
  - **Why the 138 loads stay unread.** 35 of AL-Go's 36 test files do assign
  `$scriptRoot` exactly once. But they assign it inside `BeforeAll` and load
  through it inside sibling `It` blocks, which the assigning block doesn't
  enclose. Pester carries `BeforeAll`'s variables into those `It` blocks; the
  text alone doesn't say so.
  - **Fork for you:** a Pester-specific rule, "a `BeforeAll`'s variables reach
  the `It` blocks of the same `Describe`", would read them.
  - No adapter wire came from this ruling, so there were no thirty to draw.
  Across all rulings the adapter gained one wire (from ruling 4, below), and I
  read it: true.

  ### 2. Tests are never working code's fallback

  When the caller isn't a test, the fallback to "the only file defining the
  name" now passes over test files. "Test" means the product's own
  `resolveArchetype`, not a new guess.

  - **AL-Go's five false calls from the oracle:** three are gone (from
  `Deliver.ps1`, `Deploy.psm1`, `RunPipeline.ps1`). Two remain, from
  `Actions/RunTests/RunTests.psm1`. The product's pattern `Tests?\.[^.]+$` calls
  `RunTests.psm1` a test, so the rule lets it fall back to the test stub.
  - **The adapter** never drew any of the five: the grammar loses the stubs.
  - **dbatools lost 4 true wires,** from `.github/scripts/gh-actions.ps1` and
  `gh-s3actions.ps1` to `public/Test-DbaInstanceName.ps1`,
  `Test-DbaTempDbConfig.ps1`, `Test-DbaBackupStorageCompatibility.ps1` and
  `Test-DbaPath.ps1`.
    - **Cause:** `archetype.ts` checks its test-directory pattern `\btests?\b`
  against the whole path, file name included. So any file named for PowerShell's
  approved verb `Test` counts as a test, and the generator already labels those
  docs `Archetype: test`.
    - That is a product fault outside my files; I left it alone.
  - **What's missing:** the adapter isn't given the workspace's configuration.
  It reads the archetype with the default configuration, so a workspace's
  overrides and sample roots aren't seen. Passing the configuration's archetype
  to the adapters needs a change in the generator's plumbing, which is yours to
  decide.

  ### 3. Shipped names

  Unchanged, as ruled.

  ### 4. `Split-Path` and written-out lists

  - **`Split-Path`:** of the script's own path (`$MyInvocation.MyCommand.Path`,
  `.Definition`, `$PSCommandPath`) it gives the script's folder; of another
  fixed path, that path's parent. A leaf, qualifier or is-absolute query is not
  read.
  - **Written-out lists:** `foreach` and `ForEach-Object` over a string, a list,
  `@()`, or a variable assigned one, read once per name.
  - **On the corpora:**
    - `Split-Path` gave the adapter its one new wire, `Tests/AppHelper.Test.ps1`
  → `Actions/AL-Go-Helper.ps1`, true in the source.
    - The written-out lists gave the oracle one wire, AL-Go's
  `Invoke-CopilotPRReview.psm1` → `scripts/GitHub.ps1`. The adapter misses it
  because of a grammar error in that file.

  ## Piece 1: where levelling stops

  Tuning has **Levelling** beside Search Patience: "While orders are tried"
  (`orders`, the default), "Then until ten change nothing" (`quiet`, B), "Every
  one the picture allows" (`all`, C, today's). The rule is in the scheduler the
  page and the layout lab share
  (`packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/branch-search.ts`), so
  `layout:lab refine` follows the page and takes `--levelling`.

  **What A does exactly:**
  1. One levelling is tried before each order, on the picture then shown, so a
  search of N orders tries at most N levellings.
  2. When no order is left to try (patience or cap), levelling ends at once. A
  list half-way through is dropped where it stands. Finishing it would be B's
  territory and would bring back the late moves you ruled out.
  3. Within one picture, each step of an outline (the outline, top or bottom,
  the column the step stands before) is tried once.
  4. After a levelling is taken, the list is made anew from the resulting
  picture, minus the steps already tried, so it goes on and is not begun again.
  It has to be remade: each candidate is "every run now level, plus one more
  join", so a list made before the move would undo the levelling just taken.
  5. An order taken (or a cycle's order) is another picture and gets a whole
  list. This is the one renewal: an order's trial is laid out with nothing
  joined, so its outlines have not been levelled at all.
  6. A resize or arrival restart empties the memory; a fold of a row keeps it.

  **Numbers, five files at the defaults:**

  | | Orders | Levellings | Moves | Price at the end |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Before (C, from the write-up) | 11 | 196 | 13 | 3,426,608 |
  | After (A, two runs of mine) | 11 | 11 | 9 | 3,427,546 |

  A's end price is the write-up's move-9 price to the digit, 0.027 % above
  today's end. I have no clean time for A: both runs had my probe poking the
  page (38.2 s at load 17 to 21, 58.4 s at load 22 to 28).

  **Browser cases** (one lane hold, 279.9 s, load 22 to 31). No spec text is
  changed yet, because I have no run of the suite's 80-order setting under A to
  write true numbers from.
  - `local-map-search.spec.ts`: all three green unedited (8.0 s, 1.3 min, 4.6
  s). The second case's comment ("27.7 s and 203 alignment trials") is now
  stale; by the rule it is four levellings per competition, not yet read off a
  run.
  - `local-map-hidden.spec.ts`: "pauses while the page is hidden" green (24.5
  s); the 3D pinning case green (25.2 s).
  - `local-map-hidden.spec.ts`, red for load alone: "the search pauses while
  another view is shown" (40.4 s) and "a change made while another view is
  shown" (35.7 s). Both timed out in `open()` on the 20 s wait for the first
  picture, before any search ran; load was 31 and the layout alone reported
  6,898 ms. Not rerun.
  - `local-map-simplicity.spec.ts`: not run. I had begun editing piece 2, so the
  suite rightly refused the stale bundle (15.6 s of lane wasted; my
  sequencing). I changed one line there so the lab is asked for the page's
  default levelling.
  - Unit: 1,231 client tests green, the lab's green, `npm run build` green,
  `slopcop:symbols` clean, Live Docs regenerated and committed with each commit.

  ## Piece 3: responsiveness, first figures only

  Five files, Chromium 1,440 by 900 as the suite launches it. The probe makes
  pointer moves across the map, a Shift press, a small wheel turn in and back,
  and a 2-second drag, all mixed together. Only the default (A, Applied) and a
  search-off baseline were measured.

  | | Search off | A, run 1, searching | A, run 1, after | A, run 2, searching |
  A, run 2, after |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Load (start to end of run) | 35 to 21 | 17 to 21 | same run | 22 to 28 |
  same run |
  | First picture after opening | 20.7 s | 13.3 s | | 36.0 s | |
  | Share of time in long tasks | 60 % | 84 % | 66 % | 90 % | 69 % |
  | Long task median / p95 / max, ms | 128 / 1,599 / 3,063 | 225 / 1,528 / 2,481
  | 99 / 1,453 / 4,255 | 290 / 1,824 / 3,714 | 113 / 1,796 / 7,006 |
  | Pointer move, handler delay median / p95, ms | 24 / 2,414 | 24 / 1,662 | 25
  / 2,464 | 52 / 1,623 | 25 / 3,683 |
  | Wheel turn, handler delay median, ms (n) | 549 (16) | 2,718 (4) | 701 (16) |
  3,523 (4) | 879 (8) |
  | Key press, handler delay median, ms (n) | 516 (8) | 1,394 (2) | 560 (8) |
  847 (2) | 859 (4) |
  | Browser's Event Timing, duration median / p95, ms | 4,240 / 7,000 | 4,360 /
  7,096 | 4,640 / 8,000 | 3,336 / 5,488 | 5,704 / 12,128 |
  | Drag, frames a second (longest gap) | 28.7 (1.2 s) and 13.3 (5.9 s) | 13.5
  (1.6 s) | miscounted | 5.6 (4.7 s) | 21.1 (4.0 s) |

  - **The page stutters badly while it searches.** Long tasks fill 84 to 90 % of
  the time, a wheel turn is answered about 3 seconds late, and a drag runs at 6
  to 14 frames a second with gaps over a second.
  - **It also stutters with the search off.** On this 98-card map at these
  loads, the same inputs fill 60 to 69 % of the time with long tasks, and the
  browser's own event timing is about 4 seconds either way. The search makes a
  bad state worse; it is not the only cause.
  - **These figures measure a starved machine as much as the page.** Load was 17
  to 38 on 20 threads, wheel and key counts are tiny, and the inputs' own costs
  colour each other.
  - **Not measured:** B, C, Dropped, Offered, and anything on a quiet machine. A
  keys-only run, the clean measure of queueing behind the search, was cut by
  the lane's cap. The "after" drag of run 1 is miscounted by a fault in my
  probe, since fixed.
  - **Bearing on Dropped against Offered:** as built, Offered's quiet search
  would hold the page in the searching state for up to 128 orders. I would not
  compare it fairly until it yields to the person (a trial only when pointer and
  keys have been still a moment) or leaves the main thread. That is not built;
  say if you want it.

  ## Piece 2: Late Moves

  Tuning has **Late Moves** (Applied, the default; Dropped; Offered) and **Late
  After** (0.5 to 10 s, 2.5). The while is counted from each picture's drawing
  and begins again on a resize or arrival restart.
  - **Dropped:** once late, the search is over and the root says
  `data-search-status="dropped"`. A trial that finishes after the while is let
  go.
  - **Offered:** the search turns quiet when the while is over, or sooner if the
  visitor's search has nothing left. Quiet means no patience, 128 orders in
  all, every trial priced without churn, no cycle's order taken, and moves made
  on a held arrangement, never the page. Levelling follows A on the held
  arrangement.
  - **The press:** `toggleArrangementOffer()` on the view's API moves the
  picture to the held arrangement, measured on the page first, through the
  page's own move with the usual card held. A second press puts back the kept
  picture as it was. A fold or resize drops the way back.
  - **Threshold proposed: 2 % of the shown picture's price** (`OFFER_GAIN`). Of
  the write-up's 46 moves, 44 lowered the price by 0.45 % or less and none
  changed the picture at a glance; two lowered it by 6.5 % and 6.6 % and were
  plain to see. Nothing stands between, so the evidence cannot place the bar
  more finely than "0.5 to 6"; 2 is near the middle by ratio, on the side of
  leaving a reader alone.
  - **Reach proposed: 128 orders in all** (`OFFER_ORDERS`), a count so the
  result is the same on every machine. This rests only on the write-up's order
  77; I have made no measurement of my own for it.
  - **Missing:** the control itself (icon, lit, pressed, accessible name,
  keyboard, tooltip, announcement), its styles and its browser cases. Until it
  exists, choosing Offered in Tuning gives nothing to press. The offer is
  reported as `data-search-offer` (`none`, `ready`, `taken`) and through
  `onArrangementOffer`.
  - **Where I mean to put the control:** in the strip's first group beside Fit
  and Whole, with the 3D cue's amber and single pulse, so the 3D button's place
  stays its own. Tell me if you want it elsewhere.

  On the same tree, Node 26 writes every Live Doc, the graph and every oracle
  comparison byte for byte as Node 22 does. It generates this repository in

  Node 26, because `Math.pow` differs in the last bit there.

  ## What fails on Node 26 (the gate without the browser)

  every stage on its own; results are the same with and without the timer.

  - **Live Docs pipeline, lint, build, type-check of the tests, documentation
  links, SlopCop markdown, assets and symbols:** all passed.
  - **Unit tests: 3 failures of 2,238, on Node 26 only.** They are in
  `packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphShells.test.ts` ("a ball of
  spheres packed apart (2026-11-06, second round)"). On Node 22 that file passes
  44 of 44.
    - Each failure is a last-digit difference, for example -35.17827648034136
  against -35.17827648034135.
    - Cause: I compared Math functions bit for bit over 200,000 inputs. Only
  `Math.pow` differs between the two Nodes; for example `7 ** 1.5` gives
  18.520259177452132 on 22 and 18.520259177452136 on 26.
    - The sphere packing reaches `pow` through `spatialNodeRadius` in
  `forceGraphLayout.ts`.
    - The page itself runs on the browser's V8, not the project's Node, so only
  the test is affected. Its exact-digit expectations must be brought to Node 26,
  or allow one unit in the last place, before CI moves. That file belongs to
  the Explorer's owner, so I left it alone.
  - **Integration tests: 1 failure of 154, on Node 22 as well, so it is
  `main`'s, not Node 26's.** `repository-entries.test.ts` reports
  `packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/network-simplex.systems.json` as

  ## The timer: no reason left once Node 22 is gone

  - **On Node 26 it does nothing useful.** This repository is equal within
  noise. On `dbatools` it looked a little worse: medians of 40.7 against 34.1 s,
  and 45 against 38 processor-seconds, possibly noise.
  - **The stall is a Node 22 fault, gone in Node 24.0.0.** In 24 and later, Node
  only waits on user-blocking background work. The trial agrees: there was no
  stall on 26 in any run.
  - **Hence the revert.** The loader's Live Doc keeps the cause and points at
  the probe, in case the stall ever returns.

  ## The C# condition on the commit: the timer does not slow C#

  The earlier slowdown was measurement order under load, not the timer.

  - **Back to back, on Node 22,** the 2,000-file C# corpus took:
    - without the timer: 403.7, 491.0 and 463.3 s (loads 11.6, 24.3, 10.6);
    - with it: 633.4, 556.2 and 570.5 s (loads 20.8, 13.2, 18.6).

    The extra time fell in the analysis pass. For C# that pass is pure
  JavaScript: its facts are cached from the first pass, so nothing is parsed
  again.
  - **Side by side, at the same moment under the profiler,** the two are equal:
  685 against 679 s wall, 615 against 609 processor-seconds, and 242.5 against
  239.4 s in the C# adapter's analysis pass.
  - **On 400 C# files,** three pairs show no difference (26.1–28.6 s against
  22.7–30.6 s).
  - **The C# grammar has no slow optimizing compile;** its largest takes about
  300 ms.
  - **`Dnn.Platform` crashed every generation** in `renderLiveDoc` with "Maximum
  call stack size exceeded" in `pushSection` (`document.js`). It does so at
  `cca4c405` and at `0bb8bbbc`. So I could only time its index pass, and the
  analysis up to the crash:
    - without the timer: 86.8 and 154.4 s;
    - with it: 92.3 and 141.2 s.

    The first pair was lost to the crash, which my runner hid. This crash may be
  worth sending to whoever owns `document.ts`.
  - **Something more important for the .NET work:** the C# adapter's own
  JavaScript dominates a large C# generation.
    - In the 2,000-file profile, `toDependencies` in `csharp.js` took 307 s and
  `isWorkspaceNamespace` 173 s of self time, out of about 600 s.
    - 400 files need about 15 s for the first pass; 2,000 need 250–410 s. The
  cost grows much faster than the number of files.

  **Where it is.** Commit `e36cad08c` on branch
  `worktree-agent-a39ded1f924ab596a`. It was reset to `6ac37bf2` first, nothing

  paces/Live-Documentation/.claude/worktrees/agent-a39ded1f924ab596a/AI-Agent-Wo
  rkspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-19/Probes/execution-surface/`: a
  README, plus the scripts and the `shims/` folder.
  - No product code was changed. `slopcop:markdown` and `slopcop:symbols` pass.
  - I used no subagent. The browser lane was held four times (about 3 s, 56 s,
  17 s and 81 s, all under the cap), plus one 9 s hold for the comparison.
  - One command was refused by the worktree-isolation guard: a compound command
  that would have tried an install without TypeScript. Per the rules, I did not
  retry it.
  - Load averages ran from 11 to 34 the whole time.
  - I removed my scratch files: the corpora, the install, the built page and the
  logs. I left the `/tmp/xs-*` folders alone, because I can't tell mine from
  other agents'.

  **The method.** I bundled the unchanged generator into one page with esbuild.
  Stand-ins in `shims/` replace Node's file system (an in-memory tree filled
  from a folder), `path` (pathe), `glob` (minimatch over the listing),
  `child_process` (always "no git"), and tree-sitter (handed the grammar bytes
  instead of a path).
  - All grammars the readers load are inlined, gzipped as base64, and inflated
  by the browser's `DecompressionStream`.
  - The page's content policy is `default-src 'none'; script-src 'sha256-…'
  'wasm-unsafe-eval'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; base-uri 'none'; form-action
  'none'`. That is one inline script admitted by its hash, with no `connect-src`
  at all, so the page fetches nothing.

  ## Part 1: what the generator needs from Node (read from the code)

  **Already has a plain browser equivalent:**
  - `node:path`, used in about 30 files.
  - `node:url`.
  - `typescript` (the engine only calls `createSourceFile`), `minimatch`,
  `ignore` and `smol-toml`.
  - `web-tree-sitter`, once it is given bytes instead of a path.
  - `node:crypto` and `node:zlib`, history only; the browser versions are
  asynchronous.

  **Needs a seam** (an interface the engine reads files through):
  - Reading a file's text: about 30 call sites.
  - Synchronous "is this a file" checks: 21 calls in 7 files. A browser folder
  handle only answers asynchronously, so these must be answered from a listing
  taken once at the start.
  - Asynchronous `stat` and `access` checks: 9.
  - Modification times for parse caches: 6.
  - Walks of the folder (`glob`): 9. The C#, Go, Java and Rust readers each walk
  the whole folder again.
  - The binary sniff that reads a file's first bytes: 1.
  - Writing docs, pruning old ones, and writing and re-reading the index.
  - `treeSitter.ts` finding grammars through `__dirname` and `require.resolve`.

  **The reading step a builder is adding.** It is `readSourceText` in commit
  `ab9e346d`, on branch `worktree-agent-aabeca6131a64c7b7`, not yet on `main`.
  - It routes 16 readers through one function, and its decoding half
  (`decodeSourceText`) already runs in a browser.
  - Still outside it: the C#, PowerShell, SQL, ASP.NET, project and .NET config
  readers, and the C# dependency reader.
  - So it covers the largest kind of call, reading text, but none of the
  existence checks, walks or writes. A full seam would be that function plus
  three methods: list, is-file and write.

  **Cannot be had in a page at all:**
  - **What git ignores** (`git check-ignore`). The page falls back to the root
  `.gitignore`, as the engine already does without git. Lost: nested
  `.gitignore` files, `.git/info/exclude`, and git's rule that a tracked file
  under an ignored folder is kept.
  - **`--changed`**, which asks git for status.
  - **The history engine** (about 2,500 lines). It runs git (`rev-list`,
  `read-tree`, `ls-tree` and others), builds a scratch tree per commit using
  symlinks, and `fork()`s worker processes. In a page it would mean reading
  git's packfiles in JavaScript: a second history engine, not a seam.
  - "The file maps today's state; history stays a command-line feature" needs
  none of this. An untested middle form: the command line computes history once
  and writes it into the file, which the page only reads.

  ## Part 2: whether a page can do it (measured in headless Chromium, Playwright
  1.58.2)

  - **Grammars:** all 8 `.wasm` files (the runtime and seven grammars) load
  under that policy, decoding in 0.39 to 0.65 s. One change was needed:
  tree-sitter's loader must be handed a `locateFile` function, or it tries to
  build a URL from `import.meta.url` and throws.
  - **What each origin offers:**
    - From `file://`: `showDirectoryPicker`, `showSaveFilePicker` and
  `webkitdirectory` all exist, and `isSecureContext` is true. The browser's
  private file system throws a `SecurityError`.
    - From `http://127.0.0.1`: all of these work.
  - **The native picker cannot be pressed by automation.** Called without a
  gesture, headless Chromium answered `AbortError`. Called from a click,
  Playwright's own interception aborted it, and no event fired that could hand
  it a folder.
  - **What I used instead:**
    - (a) A real folder set on an `<input webkitdirectory>`. This reads but
  cannot write back, and it is also the path for Firefox and Safari.
    - (b) The corpus copied into the browser's private file system, then read
  and written back through a `FileSystemDirectoryHandle`. That is the same
  interface the picker returns.
    - Neither proves the picker's own prompt, or the read-write prompt Chromium
  shows for a folder a person picked; the private file system grants both
  silently.
  - **Generation results:**
    - **Estate:** 0.50 to 0.69 s in the page from either origin; the tab peaked
  at 207 to 213 MB. Node took 1.3 s (process start included) and peaked at 161
  MB.
    - **Write-back through a handle:** 39 files in 0.82 s, permission granted
  without a prompt.
    - **dbatools:** 1,973 docs in 61.9 s in the page (load 25). The tab peaked
  at 608 MB; the JS heap was 411 MB after the run. Node took 59.8 to 82.4 s and
  peaked at 497 to 597 MB.
  - **The page writes exactly what Node writes.** On the estate, the page's 39
  files (from `file://`) matched Node's 39 files line for line, apart from the
  `Generated At` stamp.

  ## Part 3: what one file weighs and how it copies itself (measured)

  **The scanning page, without the viewer: 5,557,656 bytes.**
  - Script: 3.97 MB, of which TypeScript is 3.57 MB (90%) and the engine 0.24
  MB.
  - Grammars: 13.86 MB as files, 1.58 MB embedded gzipped. The largest are C++
  at 0.56 MB (5.39 raw) and C# at 0.41 MB (5.10 raw).

  **The Explorer's own script:** 1.43 MB minified (0.69 MB its own code, 0.59 MB
  three.js), plus 6.86 MB of source map and 0.23 MB of stylesheets.

  **Viewer and scanner in one file:** about 7.2 MB plus the map's data. That is
  a sum of measured parts; I did not build it.

  **The largest savings:**
  1. Gzip the grammars: 12.3 MB saved (done in the probe).
  2. Leave the source map out of the file: 6.86 MB.
  3. Read TypeScript with its tree-sitter grammars instead of the compiler:
  −3.57 MB of script, +0.36 MB of gzipped grammars. The sizes are measured; that
  the swap is complete is my reading.
  4. Gzip the script itself: 3.97 → 1.15 MB. But a script inflated at run time
  cannot be admitted by its hash, so the policy would have to allow `blob:`
  scripts or `'unsafe-eval'`. I list it only to show that price.

  **Copying itself:**
  - From both origins, the page downloaded a blank copy of itself (its own
  markup with the map's data block emptied, 5,557,646 bytes).
  - That copy, opened from disk, started with no errors.
  - The policy needed nothing extra for this: a download is not a fetch.
  - `showSaveFilePicker` exists on both origins but needs a person.
  - I did not separately run a copy holding a map.

  ## Part 4: the command line without this checkout

  **Packing today's packages:**
  - `npm pack`: engine 0.80 MB packed / 4.37 MB unpacked; generator 0.06 / 0.25
  MB; explorer 0.06 / 0.21 MB.
  - Installed with production dependencies only: **100 MB, 41 packages.** The
  largest are typescript 23, three.js 23, `@vscode/tree-sitter-wasm` 22 and
  esbuild 11 MB.
  - **`generate` works** from that install.
  - **`visualize` fails**: "Could not resolve …/explorer/dist/client/index.ts".
  The Explorer is compiled from TypeScript source on the user's machine, and the
  package carries no source. That compile step is also the only reason three.js
  and esbuild get installed.
  - **No command ships.** The commands in `scripts/live-docs/*.ts` belong to no
  package and run under `tsx`, a development dependency.

  **Other forms:**
  - **One bundled script** (generator, engine and packages): 4.05 MB measured.
  With the grammars as files and a pre-compiled Explorer it is about 19.6 MB, or
  about 7.2 MB with gzipped grammars. That total is a sum, and I did not run
  it, because of the grammar-path seam.
  - **A single executable from Node's own facility:** Node's binary here is
  124.9 MB (Linux, v22.23.5), so the result would be about 130 to 145 MB. I did
  not build it or measure the Windows binary.
  - **A package with the Explorer compiled before shipping:** about 64 MB. This
  is my reading, built from measured parts.

  ## Part 5: memory (measured; default configuration; every run a first run)

  | Folder | Default heap | 2 GB cap | 1 GB cap |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Estate | 1.3 s, 161 MB | same | same |
  | dbatools (1,973 files) | 69.0 s, 597 MB (heap peak 311 MB) | 59.8 s, 596 MB
  | 82.4 s, 497 MB (load 25.9) |
  | This repository's code (1,091 files) | 54.7 s, 486 MB | not run | 41.3 s,
  455 MB |

  - Every run finished.
  - "This repository's code" is a copy without `AI-Agent-Workspace`,
  `node_modules`, `dist` and `.git`, run under the default configuration rather
  than `.live-docs.config.json`, so it is not the committed map.
  - **Not measured:** a 512 MB cap, and the Explorer page's own memory when
  opening each map. The scanning page sits at about 188 MB when idle.

  ## What each candidate would take

  **1. The single file that scans.**
  - Pieces in the code:
    - The file-system interface (list, read bytes, is-file answered from the
  listing, write), with `readSourceText` as the first piece.
    - A grammar source that hands over bytes.
    - The generator able to keep its output in memory and write it out through
  the interface or as a download.
    - Discovery's no-git path made as good as it can be by reading nested
  `.gitignore` files through the handle.
    - Viewer and scanner in one file under a hashed policy plus
  `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`.
    - Optionally, the TypeScript swap.
  - To install: nothing; it needs Edge or Chrome to pick a folder and write into
  it. The file is about 7 MB plus its map.
  - What it cannot do:
    - Anything that runs git: history, `--changed`, the tracked-file rule.
    - Work without a person pressing the picker once and allowing writes.
    - Write beside the code in Firefox or Safari.
    - Use the browser's private file system when opened from disk.

  **2. The single file that only views and saves itself, with scanning by
  command line.**
  - Pieces in the code: the inlined Explorer, data and board under a hashed
  policy, a self-save (the probe's version is about ten lines), and something to
  read history written into the file.
  - Weight: about 1.7 MB plus the map.
  - What it cannot do: map a folder by itself.

  **3. The packaged command line.**
  - Pieces in the code: a command entry inside a package, the Explorer compiled
  at build time, and grammars found beside a bundle.
  - To install: Node 22 or later (I did not measure the size of Node's Windows
  installer), plus about 64 MB as a package or 7 to 20 MB as one script. As a
  single executable, about 130 to 145 MB and nothing else.
  - What it cannot do: run without Node, unless it is the executable.

  ## Surprises (measured)

  - The unchanged generator runs in a page and writes byte-for-byte what Node
  writes.
  - The grammars shrink about ninefold under gzip.
  - Most of October's "11 MB of Explorer scripts" is the 6.86 MB source map.
  - The TypeScript compiler is now 90% of a scanning page's script.
  - The packed command line installs three.js and esbuild only to compile the
  Explorer on the user's machine, and fails at that step.
  - From `file://`, the browser's private file system is refused while the
  folder picker is offered.
  - dbatools' readers read each file about four times: 7,676 reads for 2,002
  files.

  ## Measured versus read from the code

  Measured: every number above unless marked otherwise, the page's identical
  output, the content policy, and what each origin offers.

  My reading, not run:
  - that a page could read git's index and objects;
  - that `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` is required (Chromium's documented rule; I did not
  test without it);
  - the cost of a gzipped script;
  - the 64 MB package;
  - the single executable's size;
  - that nothing loads without `typescript`;
  - what Chromium asks when a person picks a folder and allows writing.

  I make no recommendation.

  ## SQL reader, second leg: item 1 done, item 2 answered, item 3 not started

  I did items 1 and 2. Item 3 (PL/SQL) is not started: I found a parser and a
  codebase for it, and built and measured nothing. Your four rulings are
  respected: nothing here depends on `44c8dcfe`, and `411dc559` waits for the
  names branch's `)` mend.

  Four commits on the branch, on top of `784a4082`; none amended, none pushed.
  The probe README is brought level under "The second leg".
  - `df05e35b`: the oracle reads executed strings.
  - `411dc559`: the reader reads executed strings.
  - `e9fe1ca5`: the record.
  - `c07205b0`: the gate note.

  ### 1. SQL executed from strings written out in full
  - **Oracle (`df05e35b`).** The emitter now also records:
    - `OPENQUERY` with its linked server (and `EXEC (…) AT` one);
    - whether each executed string runs in the script or inside a module's body;
    - the line the string starts on, with nested strings carrying lines of the
  file.

    The comparison reads every fully literal string ScriptDom can parse. It
  grades `ALTER PROCEDURE/FUNCTION/VIEW/TRIGGER` as a use, and places an object
  the script creates and then alters at its last ALTER. A new fixture,
  `tests/integration/fixtures/sql-server/Procedures/Billing.usp_Settle.sql`,
  covers the cases: stub then ALTER, literal and computed strings, `OPENQUERY`
  to `ORACLE_CENTRAL`, and an ALTER of a view nothing creates. Its register
  starts with the reader's 5 misses.
  - **Reader (`411dc559`).** It now reads literal `EXEC ('…' + '…')`,
  `sp_executesql N'…'`, `OPENQUERY(server, '…')` and `EXEC (…) AT server`,
  nested strings included.
    - An object a string creates is the script's symbol only when the string
  runs in the script itself, not inside a module body and not on a linked
  server.
    - What a string uses is the script's use. On a linked server it is written
  `server..schema.name`.
    - A string with any computed part is not read.
    - An ALTER of something nothing creates is a use kept by name.
    - I also changed how a written name folds: database and schema are taken by
  position, so `server...name` folds to `name`.

    Two unit tests were red against the previous reader and are green, and the
  register is back to empty.
  - **The stub-then-ALTER choice.** The one symbol stands at the last ALTER,
  because that is where the body a person reads is; the stub's `CREATE … AS
  SELECT 1` is a placeholder.

  **ScriptDom's figures, before → after:**

  | Corpus | Objects | Lines right | Dependency lines | Invented |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | tSQLt | 1,497 → 1,525 of 1,535 | 1,495 → 1,525 | 937 → 969 of 969 | 6 → 0 |
  | DNN provider scripts | 4,039 → 4,052 of 4,056 | 4,034 → 4,052 | 6,655 →
  6,961 of 6,962 | 0 |
  | DNN `.sql` | 38 → 51 of 51 | | 20 → 29 of 29 | 10 → 0 |
  | Estate SQL Server | 2/2 | | 2/2 | unchanged |

  - **What is left.** The 14 objects still missing are types and one trigger,
  kinds with no symbol. The one missing dependency line is the implicit EXEC,
  `sp_changeobjectowner 'Files', 'dbo'`; this work did not make it exact.
  - **Counted, not read (tSQLt).** 112 `EXEC (…)` and 65 `sp_executesql` with
  computed parts, and 44 `EXEC @variable`.

  **Fifty new wires.** Item 1 added 347 dependency lines (322 of them links) and
  removed 16 names kept. I drew 50 with mulberry32, seed 1119, and checked five
  of them by eye in the source.
  - 41 are ALTER uses in DNN upgrade scripts: 39 links and 2 kept names.
  - 8 are uses inside strings in tSQLt, for example `tSQLt.AssertStringIn`'s
  body calling `tSQLt.Fail` and `tSQLt.TableToText`, and inserts into
  `tSQLt.Private_HostPlatform`.
  - 1 is a DELETE inside an `sp_executesql`.

  All 50 are right by the rules. One consequence comes from the matching rule,
  not the reader: DNN's upgrade scripts re-create the same procedure in many
  versions, so an ALTER links to every version that creates it. That cannot
  happen in a system of record with one file per object, which is how the owner
  describes theirs.

  ### 2. OPENQUERY to Oracle, on the estate's own shape
  - **What the estate holds today.** Only the four-part
  `ORACLE_CENTRAL..CENTRAL.ACCOUNT` in `dbo.usp_PostPayment`, and no
  `OPENQUERY`. The board's things `sqlserver` and `oracle` (`oracle` serves
  `CENTRAL.ACCOUNT`) are not tied to the name `ORACLE_CENTRAL` anywhere.
  - **Before and after.** I added a scratch copy of `dbo.usp_GetBalance` doing
  `FROM OPENQUERY(ORACLE_CENTRAL, 'SELECT … FROM CENTRAL.ACCOUNT')`.
    - Before item 1 its doc had no dependencies.
    - After, it links to `Database/Oracle/CENTRAL.ACCOUNT.sql` (contract),
  exactly like the four-part name. Scanned apart, it keeps the same written
  name, which the estate join already matches.
  - **Why it works today.** The join matches on schema and table only and
  ignores the linked-server name. It is right when `CENTRAL.ACCOUNT` is unique
  on the board.
  - **What is missing, plainly.**
    - Which database `ORACLE_CENTRAL` reaches is defined on the SQL Server
  instance (`sp_addlinkedserver`, `sp_addlinkedsrvlogin`), usually not in source
  control.
    - So the reader cannot tell two databases that both hold `CENTRAL.ACCOUNT`
  apart.
    - An unqualified name inside the query resolves by the linked login's
  default schema, which is also not in the code.
  - **What a person would declare.** One line per linked server: "linked server
  `ORACLE_CENTRAL`, as `sqlserver` sees it, is the thing `oracle`", plus its
  default schema where that matters.
    - Where it could live: the board, as a line on the thing. On `sqlserver`: `-
  Linked server: ORACLE_CENTRAL → oracle`. Or on `oracle`: `- Reached as:
  ORACLE_CENTRAL`.
    - The vision's third tier of edges already names "a linked-server query" as
  a declared edge.
    - With such a line, the join could match a `server..schema.name` only
  against that thing's doors, with declared evidence.
    - Growing the board's format is your call; nothing was built.

  ### 3. PL/SQL: not started

  - **Parser.** The ANTLR grammars-v4 PL/SQL grammar, generated with the ANTLR
  tool jar fetched into scratch and compiled with the Java 21 already on the

  The same applies to TYPE, TRIGGER, SYNONYM and SEQUENCE.

● The measurement is back on what a typical user would run: one HTML file that
